What does the CSULB Policy on Risk Management say about driver safety and vehicle use acknowledgement?
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Risk Management, section "Driver Safety and Vehicle Use Acknowledgement": 1. Driver must be always at least 18 years old and in possession of a valid California driver's license when operating a vehicle as an ASI employee. 2. Driver shall observe all rules and regulations for safe driving as defined by the State of California Motor Vehicles Department. 3. Drinking, possession of intoxicating liquor, illegal drugs in vehicles, or driving while under the influence of alcohol or drugs is prohibited.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/discover/policies/Policy%20on%20Risk%20Mgmt_9.13.23.pdf
</bot>

</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Fraud, section "Follow-up to Preliminary Notification": An incident report will include, at a minimum: The known sequence of events Internal controls compromised Discovery means Actual or estimated asset value loss, and Remedial and/or disciplinary actions taken or considered 2.1.2 If complete information for an incident report is not known within the reporting requirement period, a preliminary notification will be submitted within the required period, followed by either a complete report or progress reports within thirty days until the matter is fully reported.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/discover/policies/policy-on-fraud.pdf

Returned Checks - what are the rules at CSULB?
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Accounting, section "Returned Checks": Accounts paid with checks that are later returned for insufficient funds will be considered unpaid and will be subject to collection procedures. In addition, the offending customer will be assessed a fee to offset bank charges. Amounts remaining unpaid as a result of returned checks might also result in criminal prosecution of the responsible party under California Penal Code Section 476a.b.

I have a question about replacement checks at CSULB. What does the policy say?
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Escheatment, section "Replacement Checks": If the payee signs and returns the letter to certify that the check was never received or was lost, the check shall be replaced. The replacement check will be charged to the liability account to which the stale dated check had been accrued. The associated payee information shall be entered into the unclaimed property database so that the check does not escheat to the state.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/discover/policies/policy-on-escheatment.pdf

Tell me about university-wide academic progress rules in the Academic Progress Rules.
</user>
<bot>
According to CSULB Policy Statement 11-10 (Academic Progress Rules), section "University-wide Academic Progress Rules": Students who have missed an academic progress milestone or who have GPAs below 2.5 may not add additional degree objectives, minors, or certificates unless approved by the advisor for their current primary academic program. Students who have missed a milestone or have GPAs below 2.5 may change primary academic programs to facilitate timely progress toward their degree.

</user>
<bot>
According to the Regulation XIII: Alcohol, Drugs And Tobacco Policies, section "Medical Amnesty Policy": Student health, safety, and well-being are top priorities for CSULB. "Amnesty" offers students who proactively exhibit responsible behavior the opportunity for educational, non-disciplinary intervention for the incident. If an individual or group seeks medical attention due to a medical emergency, the Office of Student Conduct and Ethical Development will not pursue disciplinary sanctions against the student for the consumption or possession of alcohol or drugs.

Recall of a Department Chair - what are the rules at CSULB?

<bot>
According to CSULB Policy Statement 11-06 (Department Chairs, Policies and Procedures for the Appointment and Review of), section 11.0 (Recall of a Department Chair): 11.1 When there are indications that a department chair does not have the confidence of the department faculty, the dean of the college may investigate. <user>
What does the CSULB Jeanne Clery Campus Safety Act say about definitions?
</user>
<bot>
According to the Jeanne Clery Campus Safety Act, section "Definitions": Rape is the penetration, or attempted penetration, no matter how slight, of the vagina or anus with any body part or object, or oral penetration by a sex organ of another person, without the Affirmative Consent of the Complainant. Rape also includes the attempted penetration, no matter how slight, of the vagina or anus with any body part or object, or oral penetration by a sex organ of another person, without the Affirmative Consent of the Complainant, with the present ability and the intent to commit Rape. b.
Source: https://www.csulb.edu/sites/default/files/2026/documents/OEC-2026_Clery_ASR.pdf
</bot>

</user>
<bot>
According to CSULB Policy Statement 14-04 (Academic Centers and Institutes (ACI Policy)), section 6.0 (Aci Reporting and Continuation): ACIs are subject to three types of reviews: (i) each ACI is subject to an Annual Review of its operations (Annual Review); (ii) an ACI is reviewed when it seeks to extend its Term (Continuation Review); and (iii) an ACI wishing to extend its Term beyond ten years from the date of its establishment must undergo a review to determine whether the ACI should be reauthorized (Sunset Review).

Assignment of Final Course Grades - what are the rules at CSULB?
</user>
<bot>
According to CSULB Policy Statement 22-02 (Policy on Final Course Grades, Grading Procedures, and Final Assessments), section 5.0 (Assignment of Final Course Grades): 5.3. At the start of the course and in keeping with PS 11-07 (Course Syllabi and Standard Course Outlines), instructors shall include in the syllabus the types and sequence of assignments and basis for assigning course grades. 5.4. Instructors, departments, or programs shall keep a record of students' scores on each of the demonstrations of competence on which the final course grade is based for at least five years after the conclusion of the course.

<user>
Tell me about sexual misconduct in the Policy on Discrimation, Harrassment and Retaliation.
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Discrimation, Harrassment and Retaliation, section "Sexual Misconduct": A request for someone to use a condom or birth control does not, in and of itself, constitute Affirmative Consent. Affirmative Consent can be withdrawn or revoked. Consent to one form of sexual activity (or one sexual act) does not constitute consent to other forms of sexual activity. Consent given to sexual activity on one occasion does not constitute consent on another occasion.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/discover/policies/policy-on-discrimination-harassment-and-retaliation.pdf

Can you explain the "Trade-in of Property" part of the Policy on Business Property?
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Business Property, section "Trade-in of Property": Property sold shall be removed from the property records. Sale proceeds shall be recorded as a Gain/Loss on Assets Sold. 5.3 When capitalized property is traded in, property accounts will be reduced by the recorded cost of the property traded in. Replacement property will be recorded at the cost that would be required to be paid had a trade-in not occurred.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/discover/policies/policy-on-business-property.pdf
</bot>

What does the CSULB Academic Minors say about relationship of academic minors to academic majors?
</user>
<bot>
According to CSULB Policy Statement 00-04 (Academic Minors), section 2.0 (Relationship of Academic Minors to Academic Majors): 2.1 Minors are awarded only as part of a baccalaureate degree. Students may not finish a minor after they have graduated. 2.2 The minor may be in a single subject or interdisciplinary. Students may not declare or receive a minor in the same subject as the major, and the major and minor may not have the same title.

<user>
Summarize the Policy on Name Change of Schools to Colleges.
</user>
<bot>
According to CSULB Policy Statement 91-05 (Policy on Name Change of Schools to Colleges): 1. A change from school or equivalent unit to college shall be considered a change in name only. 2. The decision to change shall be left to the discretion of each individual school or equivalent unit. 3. A change from school or equivalent unit to college shall require the approval of a majority of the full-time faculty within the school or equivalent unit, as determined by a vote of that faculty within the school or equivalent unit.
Source: https://www.csulb.edu/academic-senate/policy-statement-91-05-name-change-of-schools-to-colleges-policy

<bot>

The account numbers will be formatted in such a way as to readily identify any transaction. The panel shall make a recommendation for each program being considered, giving the reasons for the recommendation.

<user>
Hazard Assessment - what are the rules at CSULB?
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Injury and Illness Prevention Plan, section "Hazard Assessment": Worksite Inspections Worksite safety inspection checklists have been created using applicable Title 8 General Safety Orders and other information that applies to our operations. Worksite safety inspections are an essential part of hazard control and an important management tool because they provide defensible proof of an active safety program and allow consistent application of safety standards over numerous and varied worksites.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/discover/policies/policy-on-injury-and-illness-prevention-plan.pdf

What does the CSULB Jeanne Clery Campus Safety Act say about CSU long beach annual security report?

According to the Jeanne Clery Campus Safety Act, section "CSU Long Beach Annual Security Report": Decision to Proceed with Complaint Based on the assessment criteria above, the Title IX Coordinator/DHR Administrator has discretion to initiate an investigation without the Complainant's participation. When the Title IX Coordinator/DHR Administrator decides to proceed with an investigation without a Complainant's participation, the Title IX Coordinator/DHR Administrator will make the Complaint on behalf of the University.
Source: https://www.csulb.edu/sites/default/files/2026/documents/OEC-2026_Clery_ASR.pdf
